L1251: **Reviewer's comments:** There appears to be measurement redundancy across some of the assessments specifically the Skindex-29 Symptom domain and ItchyQoL Symptom domain. As such, one would expect that the results from these measures would be consistent with each other. However, this was not observed which questions the reliability of these measures.
L1252: ## 3 CLINICAL OUTCOME ASSESSMENT(S)
L1253: ## Skindex-29
L1254: The Skindex-29 is a generic instrument to measure the effect of skin disease on HRQL. It is composed of 29 items assessing 3 domains: emotions, symptoms, and functioning. The items are scored on a 5-point Likert-type scale ("never," "rarely," "sometimes," "often," or "all the time"). Responses to each item are transformed to a linear scale of 100 (where 0 equals "never", 25 equals "rarely," 50 equals "sometimes," 75 equals "often," and 100 equals "all the time") for the purpose of scale score calculation (See Section D.4). The recall period is 4 weeks.
L1255: ## Clinical Outcome Assessment Review
L1256: Jing (Julia) Ju, PharmD., PhD.
L1257: BLA 761051
L1258: Mogamuluzimab (POTELIGEO®)
L1259: Skindex-29 (skin disease symptoms and impacts); FACT-G; ItchyQoL
L1260: ## FACT-G
L1261: The FACT-G is an instrument for assessing HRQL in subjects with cancer. The FACT-G consists of 27 items in the following 4 domains: Physical Well-being, Social/Family Well-being, Emotional Well-being, and Functional Well-being. The recall period is 'past 7 days.'
L1262: ## ItchyQoL
L1263: The ItchyQoL is a pruritus-specific instrument. It includes 22 pruritus-specific questions covering 3 major domains: symptoms, functioning, and emotions. The recall period is 'past 7 days' and 'past 4 weeks.'
L1264: ## Pruritus Likert Scale
L1265: The Likert Scale for pruritus evaluation uses a numbered scale from 0 to 10 to measure the level of itching for pruritus with 0 indicating "no itch" and 10 indicating "worst itch imaginable." The recall period is undefined.
L1266: ## 4 SCORING ALGORITHM
L1267: ## Skindex-29
L1268: A subscale score is the mean of a subject's responses to the items in a given scale and the composite Skindex-29 score is calculated as the average of the 3 subscale scores to measure the overall impact on QoL. Scores range from 0 to 100 with higher scores indicate a higher impact of skin disease.
L1269: ## FACT-G
L1270: The total FACT-G score is obtained by summing individual subscale scores. Response scores on negatively-phrased questions are reversed before summing. The total score ranges from 0 to 108. Higher scores for the scales and subscales indicate better HRQL.
L1271: ## ItchyQoL
L1272: The subscale scores consist of the average of the responses to the items in a given subscale. The overall score is the average of the responses to all items. The total score ranges from 22-110 and higher ItchyQoL scores indicate worse HRQL.
L1273: ## Pruritus Likert Scale
L1274: Scores range from 0 to 10 where higher scores indicate greater itch severity and lower scores indicate lesser itch severity.
L1275: ## 5 CONTENT VALIDITY
L1276: The submission did not provide evidence to support content validity of the instruments used in Study 0761-010.
L1277: ## Clinical Outcome Assessment Review
L1278: Jing (Julia) Ju, PharmD., PhD.
L1279: BLA 761051
L1280: Mogamuluzimab (POTELIGEO®)
L1281: Skindex-29 (skin disease symptoms and impacts); FACT-G; ItchyQoL
L1282: ## **Reviewer's Comments:**
L1283: ## Comments regarding Skindex-29
L1284: *On face, the Skindex-29 appears to include some concepts that might be relevant in the context of this drug development program (e.g., skin symptoms), however there may be some important concepts that are missing that should be measured since this is a generic dermatological instrument. Without patient input from this target population we cannot determine whether this instrument includes the most important concepts for this population.*
L1285: *The instructions in the Skindex-29 are not specific to CTCL, but very vague (i.e., patients are asked to report on their “skin problem”). It is unclear whether patients were trained to think about the condition that is being treated (i.e., CTCL) when answering the questions. However, this is not a critical concern for this program.*
L1286: ## Comments regarding FACT-G
L1287: *The FACT-G is a generic HRQL PRO instrument. The FACT-G is challenging to interpret because it combines disease-related symptoms, treatment-related symptoms, and disease impacts into its summary and domain scores,*
L1288: (b) (4)
L1289: *This reviewer encourages the use of instruments with well-defined and interpretable domain content.*
L1290: ## Comments regarding ItchyQoL
L1291: *The ItchyQoL is a pruritus-specific instrument. The different recall periods of ‘past week’ and ‘past 4 weeks’ in the same instrument may be susceptible to recall error. Additionally, the instruction (the following questions concern your feelings about your itchy skin condition) may be confusing when the items are for symptoms and function.*
L1292: ## Comments regarding Pruritus Likert Scale
L1293: *A single item, in principle, is acceptable to assess pruritus intensity at its worst; however, we recommend including a recall period with. The copy of the tool provided does not indicate a recall period. It is unclear what time frame the patient was recollecting when completing the assessment.*
L1294: ## 6 OTHER MEASUREMENT PROPERTIES (RELIABILITY, CONSTRUCT VALIDITY, ABILITY TO DETECT CHANGE)
L1295: This submission did not provide evidence to support other measurement properties of the instruments used in Study 0761-010.
L1296: ## 7 INTERPRETATION OF SCORES
L1297: The applicant calculated minimal important difference (MID) estimates for all PRO endpoints using a distribution-based approach. Based on the MID estimates, subjects improved by an
L1298: ## Clinical Outcome Assessment Review
L1299: Jing (Julia) Ju, PharmD., PhD.
L1300: BLA 761051
L1301: Mogamuluzimab (POTELIGEO®)
L1302: Skindex-29 (skin disease symptoms and impacts); FACT-G; ItchyQoL
L1303: amount greater than the MID threshold and were classified as responders and all other subjects were classified as non-responders.
L1304: **Reviewer's Comments:** Distribution-based methods are viewed as a supportive method to anchor-based methods. This reviewer recommends using an anchor-based approach supplemented with both cumulative distribution function and probability density function curves to generate a within-patient meaningful change score, as well as qualitative data. The applicant did not use any patient global rating scales in Study 0761-010 to serve as an anchor for their PRO instruments. However, additional analyses were requested from the applicant to use other items from the concurrent measures as anchors. However, because of the presence of missing data it makes interpretation very difficult.
L1305: Because of the missing data at Cycle 7, the applicant proposed to use earlier time points. As such, additional CDF curves at those earlier time points were requested. However, there continues to appear to be a missing data issue at those time periods.
L1306: While the CDF curves for Skindex-29 are more interpretable at Cycles 3 and 5 and show separation between the treatment arms, they should be interpreted cautiously as the item-level and domain-level data shows that the treatment effects seen in the total score of Skindex-29 may be driven by the Emotion domain and two items in the symptom domain (items 1 and 7). The positive treatment effects observed in two items (my skin hurt; my skin condition burns or stings) in the Skindex-29 symptom scale were not observed in the similar ItchyQoL symptom items. Additionally, Skindex-29 item 10 (itchy skin) and the Pruritus Likert Scale did not produce significant results.
L1307: ## 8 LANGUAGE TRANSLATION AND CULTURAL ADAPTATION
L1308: This trial was conducted in the US, Japan, and other countries. However, this submission did not report on language translation or cultural adaptation of the COA instruments.

L1384: Figure 3 displays two Kaplan-Meier survival plots. The left plot shows Progression-Free Survival (PFS) stratified by Cmin Quartile (1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th), with the probability of survival decreasing over time (0 to 800 days). The right plot shows PFS stratified by AUC Quartile (1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th), also showing decreasing survival probability over time. Both plots indicate no clear separation between quartiles, supporting the conclusion of no exposure-response relationship for PFS.

L1404: Figure 22 displays four plots illustrating the dose proportionality of pharmacokinetic (PK) parameters after four repeated doses of IV Mogamulizumab Once Weekly, analyzed using a Power Model. The plots are arranged in a 2x2 grid, comparing linear and log-linear relationships between the PK parameter and the Log (Dose).
Top Left: Cmax 4th Dose vs Dose (Linear plot). The Y-axis (Cmax 4th Dose) ranges from 0 to 50000. The X-axis (Dose) ranges from 0.0 to 1.0. Data points show a non-linear relationship, increasing rapidly with dose.
Top Right: Log(Cmax 4th Dose) vs Log (Dose) (Log-linear plot). The Y-axis (Log(Cmax 4th Dose)) ranges from 2 to 6. The X-axis (Log (Dose)) ranges from -3 to 1. Data points show a strong positive linear correlation, indicating dose proportionality.
Bottom Left: AUC0-7 days 4th Dose vs Dose (Linear plot). The Y-axis (AUC0-7 days 4th Dose) ranges from 0 to 5000000. The X-axis (Dose) ranges from 0.0 to 1.0. Data points show a non-linear relationship, increasing rapidly with dose.
Bottom Right: Log(AUC0-7 days 4th Dose) vs Log (Dose) (Log-linear plot). The Y-axis (Log(AUC0-7 days 4th Dose)) ranges from 3 to 7. The X-axis (Log (Dose)) ranges from -3 to 1. Data points show a strong positive linear correlation, indicating dose proportionality.
